Supplementary Material

“Lossless” Compression of Deep Neural Networks:

A High-dimensional Neural Tangent Kernel Approach

Appendix A
Proofs of theorems and auxiliary results

Here, we provide the mathematical proofs of our technical results of Theorems
1
and
2
in Sections
A.1
and
A.2
, respectively.
In Section
A.3
, we provide further discussion on Theorems
1
and
2
in the case of single-hidden-layer NN and compare with the results in
[
4
]
.
In Section
A.4
we provide the proof of Corollary
1
.
In particular, as mentioned above in Footnote
3
, we fix here the algebraic mistake in the proof of Theorem
2
from the
online version
of this paper in the NeurIPS 2022 proceeding.

A.1
Proof of Theorem
1

In this section, we provide the detailed proof of Theorem
1
.
Before going into details of the proof, we first recall our system model and working assumptions as follows.

We consider
n
data vectors
\mathbf{x}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{x}_{n}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
independently drawn from one of the
K
-class Gaussian mixtures
\mathcal{C}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{C}_{K}
and denote
\mathbf{X}=[\mathbf{x}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{x}_{n}]\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p\times n}
, with class
\mathcal{C}_{a}
having cardinality
n_{a}
; that is, for
\mathbf{x}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{a}
we have

\mathbf{x}_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{a}/\sqrt{p},\mathbf{C}_{a}/p),

(20)

for mean vector
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{a}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
and non-negative definite covariance
\mathbf{C}_{a}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p\times p}
associated with class
\mathcal{C}_{a}
.

We position ourselves in the high-dimensional and non-trivial classification regime as stated in Assumption
1
, that is: As
n\to\infty
, we have, for
a\in\{1,\ldots,K\}
that,

(i)

p/n\to c\in(0,\infty)
and
n_{a}/n\to c_{a}\in(0,1)
; and

(ii)

\|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{a}\|=O(1)
; and

(iii)

for
\mathbf{C}^{\circ}\equiv\sum_{a=1}^{K}\frac{n_{a}}{n}\mathbf{C}_{a}
and
\mathbf{C}_{a}^{\circ}\equiv\mathbf{C}_{a}-\mathbf{C}^{\circ}
, we have
\|\mathbf{C}_{a}\|=O(1)
,
\tr\mathbf{C}_{a}^{\circ}=O(\sqrt{p})
and
\tr(\mathbf{C}_{a}\mathbf{C}_{b})=O(p)
for
a,b\in\{1,\ldots,K\}
; and

(iv)

\tau_{0}\equiv\sqrt{\tr\mathbf{C}^{\circ}/p}
converges in
(0,\infty)
.

Remark 4

(Beyond Gaussian mixture data)
.

Despite derived here for Gaussian mixture data, we conjecture that our results hold more generally beyond the Gaussian setting.
As concrete examples, many results in random matrix theory and high dimensional statistics such as the popular Marc̆enko-Pastur law
[
44
]
, the semicircular law
[
59
]
, as well as the circular laws
[
56
]
, have all been shown
universal
in the sense that they do
not
depend on the distribution of the (independent entries of the) data, as long as they are normalized to have zero mean and unit variance.
In a machine learning (ML) context, such
universal
behavior are observed to hold beyond the above models, and extends to
nonlinear
models such as kernel matrices
[
52
]
and neural nets
[
51
]
, in that, say, for data drawn from the family of concentrated random vectors
[
34
,
42
]
(so
not
necessarily Gaussian), the performance on those ML models are the
same
, in the larger
n,p
setting,
as if they were mere Gaussian mixtures
with the same means and covariances.
We refer the interested readers to
[
12
, Chapter 8]
for more discussions on this point.

A.1.1
Setup and notations

We consider the fully-connected neural network model of depth
L
and of successive widths
d_{1},\ldots,d_{L}
as defined in (
3
).
We further denote
\mathbf{W}_{\ell}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{\ell}\times d_{\ell-1}}
and
\sigma_{\ell}(\cdot)
the weight matrix and activation at layer
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L\}
, respectively.

We assume that the following conditions hold for the random weight matrices
\mathbf{W}_{\ell}
s and the activation function
\sigma_{\ell}
s for
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L\}
, as demanded in Assumptions
2
and
3
:

(i)

The weight matrices
\mathbf{W}_{\ell}
s are independent and have i.i.d. entries of zero mean, unit variance, and finite eight-order moment.

(ii)

The activations
\sigma_{\ell}
s are at least four-times weakly differentiable with respect to standard normal distribution, in the sense that
\max_{k\in\{0,1,2,3,4\}}\{|{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{(k)}(\xi)]|\}<C
for some universal constant
C>0
and
\xi\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
.

Our objective of interest in this section is the Conjugate Kernel (CK) matrix defined via the following recursive relation
[
28
,
8
]

[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}]_{ij}={\mathbb{E}}_{u,v}[\sigma_{\ell}(u)\sigma_{\ell}(v)],\quad\mathbf{K}_{\CK,0}=\mathbf{X}^{\sf T}\mathbf{X},

(21)

with

u,v\sim{\mathcal{N}}\left(\mathbf{0},\begin{bmatrix}[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}&[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}\\
[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}&[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{jj}\end{bmatrix}\right).

(22)

The derivation and discussion of the closely related neural tangent kernel (NTK) matrix is given in Section
A.2
.

Let Assumptions
1
–
3
hold, and let
\tau_{0},\tau_{1},\ldots,\tau_{L}\geq 0
be a sequence of non-negative numbers recursively defined via

\tau_{\ell}=\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{2}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]},

(23)

as in (
7
).
Further assume that the activation functions
\sigma_{\ell}(\cdot)
s are “centered” such that
{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]=0
.
Then, to prove Theorem
1
it suffices to show that,

(i)

the CK matrix
\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}
of layer
\ell\in\{0,1,\ldots,L\}
defined in (
21
) satisfies

\|\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}-\tilde{\mathbf{K}}_{\CK,\ell}\|\to 0,

(24)

almost surely as
n,p\to\infty
, with
\tilde{\mathbf{K}}_{\CK,\ell}
taking the generic form of

\tilde{\mathbf{K}}_{\CK,\ell}\equiv\alpha_{\ell,1}\mathbf{X}^{\sf T}\mathbf{X}+\mathbf{V}\mathbf{A}_{\ell}\mathbf{V}^{\sf T}+\alpha_{\ell,0}\mathbf{I}_{n},\quad\alpha_{\ell,0}=\tau_{\ell}^{2}-\tau_{0}^{2}\alpha_{\ell,1},

(25)

for all
\ell\in\{1\,\ldots,L\}
,
\mathbf{J}=[\mathbf{j}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{j}_{K}]\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times K}
, second-order data fluctuation vector
\boldsymbol{\psi}=\{\|\mathbf{x}_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}[\mathbf{x}_{i}]\|^{2}-{\mathbb{E}}[\|\mathbf{x}_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}[\mathbf{x}_{i}]\|^{2}]\}_{i=1}^{n}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, second-order discriminative statistics
\mathbf{t}=\{\tr\mathbf{C}_{a}^{\circ}/\sqrt{p}\}_{a=1}^{K}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{K}
,
\mathbf{T}=\{\tr\mathbf{C}_{a}\mathbf{C}_{b}/p\}_{a,b=1}^{K}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{K\times K}
, and

\mathbf{V}=[\mathbf{J}/\sqrt{p},~\boldsymbol{\psi}]\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times(K+1)},\quad\mathbf{A}_{\ell}=\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{\ell,2}\mathbf{t}\mathbf{t}^{\sf T}+\alpha_{\ell,3}\mathbf{T}&\alpha_{\ell,2}\mathbf{t}\\
\alpha_{\ell,2}\mathbf{t}^{\sf T}&\alpha_{\ell,2}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(K+1)\times(K+1)};

(26)

(ii)

the coefficients
\alpha_{\ell,1},\alpha_{\ell,2},\alpha_{\ell,3}
are non-negative and satisfy the following recursive relations

\displaystyle\alpha_{\ell,1}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,1},\quad\alpha_{\ell,2}={\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,2}+\frac{1}{4}{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2},

\displaystyle\alpha_{\ell,3}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,3}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,1}^{2}.

with
\alpha_{\ell,4}=\alpha_{\ell-1,4}{\mathbb{E}}\left[(\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi))^{2}+\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\right]
for
\xi\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
.

We first introduce the following notations that will be consistently used in the proof: for
\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{x}_{j}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
with
i\neq j
, let

\mathbf{x}_{i}=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}/\sqrt{p}+\mathbf{z}_{i}/\sqrt{p},\quad\mathbf{x}_{j}=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{j}/\sqrt{p}+\mathbf{z}_{j}/\sqrt{p},

(27)

so that
\mathbf{z}_{i}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{C}_{i})
,
\mathbf{z}_{j}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{C}_{j})
, and

\displaystyle\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{x}_{j}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}}_{O(p^{-1/2})}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{p}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}^{\sf T}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{j}+\frac{1}{p}(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}+\boldsymbol{\mu}_{j}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{i})}_{O(p^{-1})},

\displaystyle t_{i}

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{p}\tr\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}=O(p^{-1/2}),\quad\psi_{i}=\frac{1}{p}\|\mathbf{z}_{i}\|^{2}-\frac{1}{p}\tr\mathbf{C}_{i}=O(p^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle\tau_{0}

\displaystyle\equiv\sqrt{\frac{1}{p}\tr\mathbf{C}^{\circ}}=O(1),

\displaystyle\chi_{i}

\displaystyle\equiv\underbrace{t_{i}+\psi_{i}}_{O(p^{-1/2})}+\underbrace{\|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}\|^{2}/p+2\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{i}/p}_{O(p^{-1})}=\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|^{2}-{\tau_{0}}^{2},

where we note that the notations
\tau_{0},\psi_{i}
and
t_{i}
(with a slight abuse of notation to denote
\mathbf{C}_{i}=\mathbf{C}_{a}
for
\mathbf{x}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{a}
) are in line with those defined in Assumptions
1
and Theorem
1
, and we denote
S_{ij}

any
term of the form

S_{ij}\equiv S_{ij}(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})=\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\left(\gamma_{1}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})+\gamma_{2}(t_{j}+\psi_{j})\right),

(28)

for random
or
deterministic scalars
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}=O(1)
(with high probability when being random).

Remark 5
.

The introduction of
S_{ij}
will, as we shall see below, greatly simplify our analysis.
Notably, we have
S_{ij}=O(p^{-1})
and more importantly, it leads to, in matrix form, a matrix of spectral norm order
O(p^{-1/2})
, see
[
11
]
.
This (somewhat counterintuitive) spectral norm result will be consistently exploited in the remainder of the proof.

To prove the above results, it suffices to establish the following entry-wise approximation of both the diagonal and non-diagonal entries of
\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}
, the proof of which is given in Section
A.1.2
below.

Lemma 1
.

Under the assumptions and notations of Theorem
1
, for centered activation such that
{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]=0
with
\xi\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
as in the statement of Theorem
1
, we have, for all
\ell\in\{0,\ldots,L\}
and
\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}
defined in (
21
) that uniformly over all
i\neq j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
,

[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}]_{ij}=\alpha_{\ell,1}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{x}_{j}+\alpha_{\ell,2}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})(t_{j}+\psi_{j})+\alpha_{\ell,3}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2}),

(29)

and

[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}]_{ii}=\tau_{\ell}^{2}+\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\chi_{i}+\alpha_{\ell,5}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}+O(p^{-3/2}),

(30)

for
\tau_{\ell}=\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{2}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]}
and non-negative scalars
\alpha_{\ell,1},\alpha_{\ell,2},\alpha_{\ell,3},\alpha_{\ell,4},\alpha_{\ell,5}
satisfy the following recursion

\displaystyle\alpha_{\ell,1}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,1},\quad\alpha_{\ell,2}={\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,2}+\frac{1}{4}{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2},

\displaystyle\alpha_{\ell,3}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,3}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,1}^{2},

\displaystyle\alpha_{\ell,4}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{\ell-1,4}{\mathbb{E}}\left[(\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi))^{2}+\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\right],

\displaystyle\alpha_{\ell,5}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{\ell-1,5}{\mathbb{E}}\left[(\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi))^{2}+\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{4}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)+4\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)+3(\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi))^{2}\right],

with initializations

\alpha_{0,1}=1,\quad\alpha_{0,2}=0,\quad\alpha_{0,3}=0,\quad\alpha_{0,4}=1,\quad\alpha_{0,5}=0.

(31)

By Lemma
1
, we have in particular

[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}]_{ii}=\tau_{\ell}^{2}+O(p^{-1/2}),

(32)

so that in matrix form (by using the fact that
\|\mathbf{A}\|\leq n\|\mathbf{A}\|_{\max}
for
\mathbf{A}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n}
with
\|\mathbf{A}\|_{\max}=\max_{ij}|\mathbf{A}|_{ij}
and
\{S_{ij}\}_{i,j}=O_{\|\cdot\|}(p^{-\frac{1}{2}})
, see again Remark
5
, as well as
\{(\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}/p)^{2}\}_{i\neq j=1}^{n}=\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{J}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{J}^{\sf T}+O_{\|\cdot\|}(p^{-1/2})
, see
[
11
,
30
]
):

\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}=\alpha_{\ell,1}\mathbf{X}^{\sf T}\mathbf{X}+\mathbf{V}\mathbf{A}_{\ell}\mathbf{V}^{\sf T}+(\tau_{\ell}^{2}-\tau_{0}^{2}\alpha_{\ell,1})\mathbf{I}_{n}+O_{\|\cdot\|}(p^{-1/2}),

(33)

where
O_{\|\cdot\|}(p^{-1/2})
denotes matrices of spectral norm order
O(p^{-1/2})
, with

\mathbf{V}=[\mathbf{J}/\sqrt{p},~\boldsymbol{\psi}]\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times(K+1)},\quad\mathbf{A}_{\ell}=\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{\ell,2}\mathbf{t}\mathbf{t}^{\sf T}+\alpha_{\ell,3}\mathbf{T}&\alpha_{\ell,2}\mathbf{t}\\
\alpha_{\ell,2}\mathbf{t}^{\sf T}&\alpha_{\ell,2}\end{bmatrix},

(34)

and

\mathbf{T}=\left\{\frac{1}{p}\tr\mathbf{C}_{a}\mathbf{C}_{b}\right\}_{a,b=1}^{K},\quad\mathbf{t}=\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\tr\mathbf{C}_{a}^{\circ}\right\}_{a=1}^{K},

(35)

as in the statement of Theorem
1
.
This thus concludes the proof of Theorem
1
.

A.1.2
Proof of Lemma
1

In the following, we will prove Lemma
1
by induction on
\ell\in\{0,1,\ldots,L\}
.

We start our mathematical induction by considering
\ell=0
.
In this case, we have by definition
\mathbf{K}_{\CK,0}=\mathbf{X}^{\sf T}\mathbf{X}
so that

\mathbf{K}_{\CK,0}=\tilde{\mathbf{K}}_{\CK,0}=\mathbf{X}^{\sf T}\mathbf{X},

(36)

with
\alpha_{0,1}=1
,
\alpha_{0,2}=0
,
\alpha_{0,3}=0
for non-diagonal entries and
\alpha_{0,4}=1
,
\alpha_{0,5}=0
for diagonal entries.

We then assume the entry-wise approximation in Lemma
1
holds at layer
\ell-1
with
6
6

6

Recall that the introduction of the term
S_{ij}
does
not
alter the form of
\mathbf{K}_{\CK}
or
\tilde{\mathbf{K}}_{\CK}
in a spectral norm sense, see again Remark
5
above.

[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}=\alpha_{\ell-1,1}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{x}_{j}+\alpha_{\ell-1,2}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})(t_{j}+\psi_{j})+\alpha_{\ell-1,3}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2}),

(37)

for
i\neq j
, and

[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}=\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}+\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\chi_{i}+\alpha_{\ell-1,5}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}+O(p^{-3/2}).

(38)

and work on the CK matrix
\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}
at layer
\ell
.

By definition in (
21
), using the Gram-Schmidt orthogonalization procedure for standard Gaussian random variable as in
[
18
,
4
]
, we write

u=\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}\cdot\xi_{i},\quad v=\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}}{\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}}\cdot\xi_{i}+\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{jj}-\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}^{2}}{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}}\cdot\xi_{j},

(39)

for
independent

\xi_{i},\xi_{j}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
.
As such, we have, for layer
\ell
that

\displaystyle[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}]_{ii}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}^{2}\left(\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}\cdot\xi_{i}\right)\right]

(40)

\displaystyle[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}]_{ij}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}\left(\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}\cdot\xi_{i}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\times\sigma_{\ell}\left(\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}}{\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}}\cdot\xi_{i}+\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{jj}-\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}^{2}}{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}}\cdot\xi_{j}\right)\right],

(41)

where the expectations are now taken with respect to the
independent
random variables
\xi_{i}
and
\xi_{j}
(and in fact, conditioned on the random vectors
\mathbf{x}_{i}
and
\mathbf{x}_{j}
).

The objective is then to derive the approximation of
[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}]_{ij}
and
[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}]_{ii}
at layer
\ell
, both to terms of order
O(p^{-3/2})
, and to subsequently derive the recursive relations between the key coefficients of layer
\ell-1
:

\{\alpha_{\ell-1,1},\alpha_{\ell-1,2},\alpha_{\ell-1,3},\alpha_{\ell-1,4},\alpha_{\ell-1,5}\},

(42)

and those of layer
\ell

\{\alpha_{\ell,1},\alpha_{\ell,2},\alpha_{\ell,3},\alpha_{\ell,4},\alpha_{\ell,5}\},

(43)

as given in the statement of Lemma
1
and Theorem
1
.

On the diagonal.

We start with the diagonal entries of
\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}
, which, as per its expression in (
40
), depends on the diagonal entries
[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}
at layer
\ell-1
as defined in (
38
).
By Taylor-expanding the nonlinear function
f(t)=\sqrt{t}
around the leading order term
t\simeq\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}=O(1)
, one gets

\displaystyle\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}+\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\chi_{i}+\alpha_{\ell-1,5}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}+O(p^{-3/2})}

\displaystyle=\tau_{\ell-1}+\frac{1}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\left(\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\chi_{i}+\alpha_{\ell-1,5}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}\right)-\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{8\tau_{\ell-1}^{3}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}+O(p^{-3/2})

\displaystyle=\tau_{\ell-1}+\frac{1}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\chi_{i}+\frac{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,5}-\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{8\tau_{\ell-1}^{3}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}+O(p^{-3/2}).

Further Taylor-expand
\sigma_{\ell}^{2}(\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}\cdot\xi_{i})=f(\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}\cdot\xi_{i})
around
\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i}
, we get

\displaystyle[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}]_{ii}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}^{2}\left(\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}\cdot\xi\right)\right]={\mathbb{E}}\left[f\left(\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}\cdot\xi\right)\right]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left[f(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)+f^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\xi\left(\frac{1}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\chi_{i}+\frac{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,5}-\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{8\tau_{\ell-1}^{3}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\xi^{2}\right]\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}+O(p^{-3/2})

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}[f(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]+{\mathbb{E}}[f^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]\left(\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\chi_{i}+\frac{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,5}-\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{8\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\xi^{2}\right]\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}+O(p^{-3/2})

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}[f(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]+\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}}{2}{\mathbb{E}}[f^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]\chi_{i}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{4\alpha_{\ell-1,5}{\mathbb{E}}[f^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]+\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}[f^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]}{8}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}+O(p^{-3/2}),

where we denote the shortcut
f(x)=\sigma_{\ell}^{2}(x)
and use the facts that

{\mathbb{E}}[f^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\xi]=\tau_{\ell-1}{\mathbb{E}}[f^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)],\quad{\mathbb{E}}[f^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)(\xi^{2}-1)]=\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}[f^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)],

(44)

for
\xi\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
, as a consequence of the Gaussian integration by parts formula.

As a consequence, we obtain the following relation

\displaystyle\tau_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{2}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]},\quad\alpha_{\ell,4}=\alpha_{\ell-1,4}{\mathbb{E}}\left[(\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi))^{2}+\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\right],

\displaystyle\alpha_{\ell,5}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{\ell-1,5}{\mathbb{E}}\left[(\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi))^{2}+\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{4}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)+4\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)+3(\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi))^{2}\right].

This concludes the proof for the diagonal entries of
\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}
in Lemma
1
.

Off the diagonal.

We now move on to the more involved non-diagonal entries of
\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}
. First note, for
i\neq j
, that

\displaystyle\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}}{\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}}{\tau_{\ell-1}+\frac{1}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\chi_{i}+\frac{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,5}-\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{8\tau_{\ell-1}^{3}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}+O(p^{-3/2})}

\displaystyle=[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{\ell-1}}-\frac{1}{\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})+O(p^{-1})\right)\right)+O(p^{-3/2})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau_{\ell-1}}[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}-\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\alpha_{\ell-1,1}}{2\tau_{\ell-1}^{3}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}+O(p^{-3/2})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau_{\ell-1}}[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2})=O(p^{-1/2}),

with

\displaystyle[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{\ell-1,1}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{x}_{j}+\alpha_{\ell-1,2}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})(t_{j}+\psi_{j})+\alpha_{\ell-1,3}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2})=O(p^{-1/2}),

as in (
37
), where we recall
S_{ij}=\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\left(\gamma_{1}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})+\gamma_{2}(t_{j}+\psi_{j})\right)=O(p^{-1})
and is of vanishing spectral norm, see again Remark
5
.

Then, it follows that

\displaystyle\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{jj}-\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}^{2}}{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}+\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\chi_{j}+\alpha_{\ell-1,5}(t_{j}+\psi_{j})^{2}-\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,1}^{2}}{\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}+O(p^{-3/2})}

\displaystyle=\tau_{\ell-1}+\frac{1}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\left(\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\chi_{j}+\alpha_{\ell-1,5}(t_{j}+\psi_{j})^{2}-\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,1}^{2}}{\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}\right)-\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}(t_{j}+\psi_{j})^{2}}{8\tau_{\ell-1}^{3}}+O(p^{-3/2})

\displaystyle=\tau_{\ell-1}+\frac{1}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\left(\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\chi_{j}-\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,1}^{2}}{\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}\right)+\frac{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,5}-\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{8\tau_{\ell-1}^{3}}(t_{j}+\psi_{j})^{2}+O(p^{-3/2}).

As a consequence, we get, again by Taylor expansion that

\displaystyle\sigma_{\ell}\left(\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}\cdot\xi_{i}\right)\sigma_{\ell}\left(\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}}{\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}}\cdot\xi_{i}+\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{jj}-\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}^{2}}{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}}\cdot\xi_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\ell}\left(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i}+\frac{1}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\chi_{i}\xi_{i}+\frac{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,5}-\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{8\tau_{\ell-1}^{3}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}\xi_{i}+O(p^{-3/2})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\times\sigma_{\ell}\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{\ell-1}}[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}\xi_{i}+\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j}+\frac{1}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\left(\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\chi_{j}-\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,1}^{2}}{\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}\right)\xi_{j}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\frac{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,5}-\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{8\tau_{\ell-1}^{3}}(t_{j}+\psi_{j})^{2}\xi_{j}+O(p^{-3/2})\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})+\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}\left(\frac{1}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\chi_{i}+\frac{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,5}-\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{8\tau_{\ell-1}^{3}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\left.+\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}^{2}\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{8\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})+\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\left(\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}}{\tau_{\ell-1}}\xi_{i}+\frac{1}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\left(\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\chi_{j}-\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,1}^{2}}{\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}\right)\xi_{j}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\left.+\frac{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,5}-\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{8\tau_{\ell-1}^{3}}(t_{j}+\psi_{j})^{2}\xi_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\left.+\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\left(\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,1}\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tau_{\ell-1}}\xi_{i}+\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}(t_{j}+\psi_{j})}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\xi_{j}\right)^{2}\right)+O(p^{-3/2})

\displaystyle\equiv\left(\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})+T_{1,i}+T_{2,i}\right)\left(\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})+T_{3,ij}+T_{3,j}+T_{4,ij}+T_{4,j}+S_{ij}\right)+O(p^{-3/2}),

where we denote the shortcuts:

\displaystyle T_{1,i}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}\cdot\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\chi_{i}=O(p^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle T_{2,i}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,5}\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}+\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}\frac{\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}^{2}\tau_{\ell-1}-\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}}{8\tau_{\ell-1}^{3}}\right)(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}=O(p^{-1}),

that
only
depend on
\xi_{i}
; and

\displaystyle T_{3,ij}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\xi_{i}\cdot\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}}{\tau_{\ell-1}}=O(p^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle T_{4,ij}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\xi_{i}^{2}\cdot\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,1}^{2}}{\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}=O(p^{-1}),

that depend on both
\xi_{i}
and
\xi_{j}
; as well as

\displaystyle T_{3,j}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\xi_{j}\cdot\left(\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\chi_{j}-\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,1}^{2}}{2\tau_{\ell-1}^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}+\frac{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,5}-\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{8\tau_{\ell-1}^{3}}(t_{j}+\psi_{j})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=O(p^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle T_{4,j}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\xi_{j}^{2}\cdot\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}}(t_{j}+\psi_{j})^{2}=O(p^{-1}),

that
only
depend on
\xi_{j}
, where we particularly note that the cross terms are of the form
S_{ij}
.

As such, we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{\ell}\left(\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}\cdot\xi_{i}\right)\sigma_{\ell}\left(\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}}{\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}}\cdot\xi_{i}+\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{jj}-\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}^{2}}{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}}\cdot\xi_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})+\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})(T_{3,ij}+T_{4,ij})+\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})(T_{3,j}+T_{4,j})

\displaystyle\quad+\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})(T_{1,i}+T_{2,i})+T_{1,i}(T_{3,ij}+T_{3,j})+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2}),

with in particular

\displaystyle T_{1,i}(T_{3,ij}+T_{3,j})

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}\cdot\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})\cdot\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\xi_{i}\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,1}\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tau_{\ell-1}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}\cdot\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})\cdot\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\xi_{j}\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}(t_{j}+\psi_{j})+O(p^{-3/2})

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\xi_{j}\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})(t_{j}+\psi_{j})+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2}).

We thus conclude, for
{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]=0
with
\xi\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
, that

\displaystyle[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}]_{ij}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}\left(\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}\cdot\xi_{i}\right)\sigma_{\ell}\left(\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}}{\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}}\cdot\xi_{i}+\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{jj}-\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}^{2}}{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}}\cdot\xi_{j}\right)\right]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})(T_{3,ij}+T_{4,ij})]+{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\xi_{j}\right]\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})(t_{j}+\psi_{j})

\displaystyle\quad+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2})

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\xi_{i}\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}}{\tau_{\ell-1}}\right]+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\xi_{i}^{2}\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,1}^{2}}{\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}]{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\xi_{j}]\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})(t_{j}+\psi_{j})+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2})

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]^{2}[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}+\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,1}^{2}}{2}{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]^{2}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{4}{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]^{2}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})(t_{j}+\psi_{j})+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2}),

where we used again the fact that

{\mathbb{E}}[\xi f(\tau\xi)]=\tau{\mathbb{E}}[f^{\prime}(\tau\xi)],\quad{\mathbb{E}}[\xi^{2}f(\tau\xi)]={\mathbb{E}}[(\xi^{2}-1)f(\tau\xi)]=\tau^{2}{\mathbb{E}}[f^{\prime\prime}(\tau\xi)],

(45)

for
{\mathbb{E}}[f(\tau\xi)]=0
.

This allows us to conclude that

\displaystyle\alpha_{\ell,1}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,1},\quad\alpha_{\ell,2}={\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,2}+\frac{1}{4}{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2},

(46)

\displaystyle\alpha_{\ell,3}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,3}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,1}^{2}.

(47)

This concludes the proof for the diagonal entries of
\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}
in Lemma
1
, and thus the proof of Lemma
1
.

Lemma 2

(Consistent estimation of
\tau_{0}
)
.

Let Assumption
1
hold and let
\tau_{0}\equiv\sqrt{\tr\mathbf{C}^{\circ}/p}
. Then, as
n,p\to\infty
with
p/n\to c\in(0,\infty)
, we have,

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\right\|^{2}-\tau_{0}^{2}\to 0,

(48)

almost surely.

Proof of Lemma
2
.

It follows from (
27
) that

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\right\|^{2}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{a=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{a}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\left\|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{a}\right\|^{2}-\frac{2}{p}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{a}^{{\sf T}}\mathbf{z}_{i}+\frac{1}{p}\left\|\mathbf{z}_{i}\right\|^{2}\right).

(49)

From Assumption
1
, we have
\|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{a}\|=O(1)
so that
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{a=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{a}}\frac{1}{p}\left\|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{a}\right\|^{2}=O\left(p^{-1}\right)
.
Since
{\mathbb{E}}[\mathbf{z}_{i}]=\mathbf{0}
, the second term
\frac{2}{p}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{a}^{{\sf T}}\mathbf{z}_{i}
of (
49
) is a weighted sum of independent zero mean random variables and vanishes with probability one as
n,p\rightarrow\infty
by a mere application of Chebyshev’s inequality and the Borel–Cantelli lemma.
Finally, using the strong law of large numbers on the third term of equation (
49
), we have almost surely,

\frac{1}{n}\frac{1}{p}\sum_{a=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{a}}\left\|\mathbf{z}_{i}\right\|^{2}=\frac{1}{p}\sum_{a=1}^{K}\frac{n_{a}}{n}\tr\mathbf{C}_{a}+o(1)=\tr\mathbf{C}^{\circ}/p+o(1),

(50)

where in the last line we use
\tr\mathbf{C}_{a}^{\circ}=O(\sqrt{p})
from Assumption
1
, and thus
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\mathbf{z}_{i}\right\|^{2}-\tau_{0}^{2}\rightarrow 0
almost surely.
This concludes the proof of Lemma
2
.
∎

A.2
Proof of Theorem
2

In this section, we provide detailed proof of Theorem
2
.
We follows the same notations and working assumptions as in the proof of Theorem
1
in Appendix
A.1
.

As already mentioned in (
6
), the NTK matrices
\mathbf{K}_{\NTK,\ell}
of layer
\ell
can be defined, again in an iterative manner, via the CK matrices
\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}
and
\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\CK,\ell}
as follows
[
28
]
:

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{\NTK,0}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{K}_{\CK,0}=\mathbf{X}^{\sf T}\mathbf{X},

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{\NTK,\ell}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}+\mathbf{K}_{\NTK,\ell-1}\circ\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\CK,\ell}.

where ‘
\mathbf{A}\circ\mathbf{B}
’ denotes the Hadamard product between two matrices
\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}
, and
\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\CK,\ell}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n}
denotes a CK matrix with nonlinear activation
\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\cdot)
instead of
\sigma_{\ell}(\cdot)
as for
\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}
in (
4
), that is

[\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\CK,\ell}]_{ij}={\mathbb{E}}_{u,v}[\sigma^{\prime}_{\ell}(u)\sigma^{\prime}_{\ell}(v)],\quad u,v\sim{\mathcal{N}}\left(\mathbf{0},\begin{bmatrix}[\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}&[\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}\\
[\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}&[\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{jj}\end{bmatrix}\right).

(51)

To prove Theorem
2
, one may want to apply directly Lemma
1
to obtain an entry-wise approximation of
\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\CK,\ell}
, with activation
\sigma^{\prime}(\cdot)
instead of
\sigma(\cdot)
.
This approach works only if one assumes both
{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]=0
and
{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]=0
hold true for
\xi\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
, which is too restrictive.

Instead, we will prove the following entry-wise approximation result on
\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\CK,\ell}
, by assuming
{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]=0
only.

Lemma 3
.

Under the assumptions and notations of Theorem
1
, for centered activation such that
{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]=0
with
\xi\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
as in the statement of Theorem
1
, we have, for all
\ell\in\{0,\ldots,L\}
and
\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\CK,\ell}
defined in (
51
), that uniformly over all
i\neq j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
,

[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}^{\prime}]_{ij}=\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,0}+\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,1}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{x}_{j}+\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,2}\left(\chi_{i}+\chi_{j}\right)+O(p^{-1}),

(52)

and
[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}^{\prime}]_{ii}=\dot{\tau}_{\ell}^{2}+O(p^{-1/2})
, for
\dot{\tau}_{\ell}=\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]}
,
\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,0}={\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\right]^{2}
, and non-negative scalars
\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,1},\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,2}
such that

\displaystyle\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,1}={\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\right]^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,1},\quad\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,2}={\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\right]{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\right]\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}}{2},

with initializations
\dot{\tau}_{0}=0
,
\dot{\alpha}_{0,0}=0
,
\dot{\alpha}_{0,1}=1
, and
\dot{\alpha}_{0,2}=0
.

The proof of Lemma
3
is built upon Lemma
1
and given as follows.

A.2.1
Proof of Lemma
3

By its definition in (
51
), we write again using the Gram-Schmidt orthogonalization procedure for standard Gaussian random variable that

u=\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}\cdot\xi_{i},\quad v=\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}}{\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}}\cdot\xi_{i}+\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{jj}-\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}^{2}}{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}}\cdot\xi_{j},

(53)

for
independent

\xi_{i},\xi_{j}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
.
As such, we have, for layer
\ell
that

\displaystyle[\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\CK,\ell}]_{ii}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}\left(\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}\cdot\xi_{i}\right)\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}\left(\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}\cdot\xi_{i}\right)\right]

(54)

\displaystyle[\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\CK,\ell}]_{ij}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}\left(\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}\cdot\xi_{i}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\times\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}\left(\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}}{\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}}\cdot\xi_{i}+\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{jj}-\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}^{2}}{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}}\cdot\xi_{j}\right)\right],

(55)

where the expectations taken with respect to the
independent
random variables
\xi_{i}
and
\xi_{j}
, as in the proof of Lemma
1
.

It follows from Lemma
1
that for
{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]=0
, we have, for layer
\ell-1
that:

[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}=\alpha_{\ell-1,1}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{x}_{j}+\alpha_{\ell-1,2}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})(t_{j}+\psi_{j})+\alpha_{\ell-1,3}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2}),

(56)

for
i\neq j
, and

[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}=\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}+\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\chi_{i}+\alpha_{\ell-1,5}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}+O(p^{-3/2}),

(57)

for non-negative scalars
\alpha_{\ell,1},\alpha_{\ell,2},\alpha_{\ell,3},\alpha_{\ell,4},\alpha_{\ell,5}
defined in Lemma
1
.

Then, the proof of Lemma
3
is akin to that of Lemma
1
,
with activation
\sigma^{\prime}
instead of
\sigma
.

On the diagonal.

Since

\displaystyle\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}+\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\chi_{i}+\alpha_{\ell-1,5}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}+O(p^{-3/2})}

\displaystyle=\tau_{\ell-1}+\frac{1}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\left(\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\chi_{i}+\alpha_{\ell-1,5}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}\right)-\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{8\tau_{\ell-1}^{3}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}+O(p^{-3/2})

\displaystyle=\tau_{\ell-1}+\frac{1}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\chi_{i}+\frac{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,5}-\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{8\tau_{\ell-1}^{3}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}+O(p^{-3/2}),

and denote the shortcut
f(t)=\left(\sigma^{\prime}(t)\right)^{2}
, we get, again by Taylor-expansion that

\displaystyle[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}^{\prime}]_{ii}={\mathbb{E}}\left[f\left(\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}\cdot\xi\right)\right]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left[f(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)+f^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\xi\left(\frac{1}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\chi_{i}+\frac{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,5}-\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{8\tau_{\ell-1}^{3}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\xi^{2}\right]\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}+O(p^{-3/2})

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}[f(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]+{\mathbb{E}}[f^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]\left(\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\chi_{i}+\frac{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,5}-\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{8\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\xi^{2}\right]\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}+O(p^{-3/2})

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}[f(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]+\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}}{2}{\mathbb{E}}[f^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]\chi_{i}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{4\alpha_{\ell-1,5}{\mathbb{E}}[f^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]+\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}[f^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]}{8}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}+O(p^{-3/2}),

where we used the facts that

{\mathbb{E}}[f^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\xi]=\tau_{\ell-1}{\mathbb{E}}[f^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)],\quad{\mathbb{E}}[f^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)(\xi^{2}-1)]=\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}[f^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)],

(58)

for
\xi\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
, again as a consequence of the Gaussian integration by parts formula.

This allows us to conclude that the proof of Lemma
3
for the diagonal entries of
\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}^{\prime}
, with
\dot{\tau}_{\ell}=\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]}
.

Off the diagonal.

We now move on to the non-diagonal entries of
\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}^{\prime}
.
First recall from Lemma
1
that for
i\neq j
,

\displaystyle\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}}{\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau_{\ell-1}}[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2})=O(p^{-1/2}),

with

\displaystyle[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{\ell-1,1}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{x}_{j}+\alpha_{\ell-1,2}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})(t_{j}+\psi_{j})+\alpha_{\ell-1,3}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2})=O(p^{-1/2}),

and therefore

\displaystyle\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{jj}-\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}^{2}}{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}}

\displaystyle=\tau_{\ell-1}+\frac{1}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\left(\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\chi_{j}-\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,1}^{2}}{\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}\right)+\frac{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,5}-\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{8\tau_{\ell-1}^{3}}(t_{j}+\psi_{j})^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+O(p^{-3/2}).

As a consequence, we get, again by Taylor expansion that

\displaystyle\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}\left(\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}\cdot\xi_{i}\right)\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}\left(\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}}{\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}}\cdot\xi_{i}+\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{jj}-\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}^{2}}{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}}\cdot\xi_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}\left(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i}+\frac{1}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\chi_{i}\xi_{i}+\frac{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,5}-\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{8\tau_{\ell-1}^{3}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}\xi_{i}+O(p^{-3/2})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\times\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{\ell-1}}[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}\xi_{i}+\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j}+\frac{1}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\left(\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\chi_{j}-\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,1}^{2}}{\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}\right)\xi_{j}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\frac{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,5}-\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{8\tau_{\ell-1}^{3}}(t_{j}+\psi_{j})^{2}\xi_{j}+O(p^{-3/2})\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})+\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}\left(\frac{1}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\chi_{i}+\frac{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,5}-\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{8\tau_{\ell-1}^{3}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\left.+\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}^{2}\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{8\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})+\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\left(\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}}{\tau_{\ell-1}}\xi_{i}+\frac{1}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\left(\alpha_{\ell-1,4}\chi_{j}-\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,1}^{2}}{\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}\right)\xi_{j}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\left.+\frac{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,5}-\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{8\tau_{\ell-1}^{3}}(t_{j}+\psi_{j})^{2}\xi_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\left.+\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\left(\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,1}\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tau_{\ell-1}}\xi_{i}+\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}(t_{j}+\psi_{j})}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\xi_{j}\right)^{2}\right)+O(p^{-3/2})

\displaystyle\equiv\left(\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})+T_{1,i}+T_{2,i}\right)\left(\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})+T_{3,ij}+T_{3,j}+T_{4,ij}+T_{4,j}+S_{ij}\right)+O(p^{-3/2}),

where we denote the shortcuts:

\displaystyle T_{1,i}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}\cdot\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\chi_{i}=O(p^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle T_{2,i}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,5}\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}+\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}\frac{\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}^{2}\tau_{\ell-1}-\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}}{8\tau_{\ell-1}^{3}}\right)(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}=O(p^{-1}),

that
only
depend on
\xi_{i}
; and

\displaystyle T_{3,ij}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\xi_{i}\cdot\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}}{\tau_{\ell-1}}=O(p^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle T_{4,ij}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\xi_{i}^{2}\cdot\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,1}^{2}}{\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}=O(p^{-1}),

that depend on both
\xi_{i}
and
\xi_{j}
; and

\displaystyle T_{3,j}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\xi_{j}\cdot\left(\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\chi_{j}-\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,1}^{2}}{2\tau_{\ell-1}^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}+\frac{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,5}-\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{8\tau_{\ell-1}^{3}}(t_{j}+\psi_{j})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=O(p^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle T_{4,j}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\xi_{j}^{2}\cdot\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}^{2}}{4\tau_{\ell-1}^{2}}(t_{j}+\psi_{j})^{2}=O(p^{-1}),

that
only
depend on
\xi_{j}
, where we particularly note that the cross terms are of the form
S_{ij}
.

As such, we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}\left(\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}\cdot\xi_{i}\right)\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}\left(\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}}{\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}}\cdot\xi_{i}+\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{jj}-\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}^{2}}{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}}\cdot\xi_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})+\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})(T_{3,ij}+T_{4,ij})+\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})(T_{3,j}+T_{4,j})

\displaystyle\quad+\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})(T_{1,i}+T_{2,i})+T_{1,i}(T_{3,ij}+T_{3,j})+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2})

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})+\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})(T_{3,ij}+O(p^{-1}))+\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})(T_{3,j}+O(p^{-1}))

\displaystyle\quad+\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})(T_{1,i}+O(p^{-1}))+O(p^{-1})+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2})

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})+\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\left(\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\xi_{i}\cdot\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}}{\tau_{\ell-1}}+O(p^{-1})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\left(\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\xi_{j}\cdot\left(\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\chi_{j}+O(p^{-1})\right)+O(p^{-1})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\left(\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}\cdot\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\chi_{i}+O(p^{-1})\right)+O(p^{-1})+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2})

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})+\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\cdot\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}}{\tau_{\ell-1}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\xi_{j}\cdot\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\chi_{j}+\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\cdot\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\chi_{i}+S_{ij}+O(p^{-1})

This further leads to the following approximation of
[\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\CK,\ell}]_{ij}
as

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}\left(\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}\cdot\xi_{i}\right)\times\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}\left(\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}}{\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}}\cdot\xi_{i}+\sqrt{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{jj}-\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}^{2}}{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ii}}}\cdot\xi_{j}\right)\right]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})+\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\cdot\frac{[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}}{\tau_{\ell-1}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\xi_{j}\cdot\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\chi_{j}+\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi_{j})\cdot\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}}{2\tau_{\ell-1}}\chi_{i}+S_{ij}+O(p^{-1})\bigg]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\right]^{2}+{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\right]^{2}\cdot[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell-1}]_{ij}

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\right]{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\right]\cdot\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}}{2}\left(\chi_{i}+\chi_{j}\right)+S_{ij}+O(p^{-1})

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\right]^{2}+{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\right]^{2}\cdot\alpha_{\ell-1,1}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{x}_{j}+{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\right]{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\right]\cdot\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}}{2}\left(\chi_{i}+\chi_{j}\right)+O(p^{-1})

\displaystyle=\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,0}+\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,1}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{x}_{j}+\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,2}\left(\chi_{i}+\chi_{j}\right)+O(p^{-1})

with
\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,0}={\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\right]^{2}
and

\displaystyle\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,1}={\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\right]^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,1},\quad\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,2}={\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\right]{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\right]\frac{\alpha_{\ell-1,4}}{2},

where we used again the Gaussian integration by parts formula.
This concludes the proof of Lemma
3
.

A.2.2
End of proof of Theorem
2

With Lemma
3
at hand, it remains to show, as in the proof of Theorem
1
in Section
A.1
, that the on- and off-diagonal entries of
\mathbf{K}_{\NTK,\ell}
satisfy the following relation:

[\mathbf{K}_{\NTK,\ell}]_{ij}=\beta_{\ell,1}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{x}_{j}+\beta_{\ell,2}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})(t_{j}+\psi_{j})+\beta_{\ell,3}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2}),

(59)

for all
i\neq j
and

[\mathbf{K}_{\NTK,\ell}]_{ii}=\kappa_{\ell}^{2}+O(p^{-1/2}),

(60)

with
\tau_{\ell}^{2}+\kappa_{\ell-1}^{2}\dot{\tau}_{\ell}^{2}
.

We again proof the above approximation by induction on
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L\}
.
For the case
\ell=0
we have
\mathbf{K}_{\NTK,0}=\mathbf{X}^{\sf T}\mathbf{X}
so that

\beta_{\ell,1}=1,\quad\beta_{\ell,2}=\beta_{\ell,3}=0,\quad\kappa_{0}=\tau_{0}.

(61)

We then assume the induction hypothesis holds for
\ell-1
, that is

\displaystyle[\mathbf{K}_{\NTK,\ell-1}]_{ij}

\displaystyle=\beta_{\ell-1,1}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{x}_{j}+\beta_{\ell-1,2}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})(t_{j}+\psi_{j})+\beta_{\ell-1,3}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2}),

\displaystyle[\mathbf{K}_{\NTK,\ell-1}]_{ii}

\displaystyle=\kappa_{\ell-1}^{2}+O(p^{-1/2}),

and work on the off-diagonal and then diagonal entries of
\mathbf{K}_{\NTK,\ell}
at the layer
\ell
as follows.

Off the diagonal.

By Lemma
1
and Lemma
3
, we have, for the non-diagonal entries of
\mathbf{K}_{\NTK,\ell}
with
i\neq j
that

\displaystyle[\mathbf{K}_{\NTK,\ell}]_{ij}

\displaystyle=[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}]_{ij}+[\mathbf{K}_{\NTK,\ell-1}]_{ij}[\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\CK,\ell}]_{ij}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{\ell,1}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{x}_{j}+\alpha_{\ell,2}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})(t_{j}+\psi_{j})+\alpha_{\ell,3}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\beta_{\ell-1,1}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{x}_{j}+\beta_{\ell-1,2}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})(t_{j}+\psi_{j})+\beta_{\ell-1,3}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,0}+\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,1}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{x}_{j}+\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,2}\cdot\left(\chi_{i}+\chi_{j}\right)+O(p^{-1})\right)+O(p^{-3/2})

\displaystyle=(\alpha_{\ell,1}+\beta_{\ell-1,1}\cdot\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,0})\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{x}_{j}+(\alpha_{\ell,2}+\beta_{\ell-1,2}\cdot\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,0})(t_{i}+\psi_{i})(t_{j}+\psi_{j})

\displaystyle\quad+(\alpha_{\ell,3}+\beta_{\ell-1,3}\cdot\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,0}+\beta_{\ell-1,1}\cdot\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,1})\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2}),

so that

\displaystyle\beta_{\ell,1}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{\ell,1}+\beta_{\ell-1,1}\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,0},

\displaystyle\beta_{\ell,2}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{\ell,2}+\beta_{\ell-1,2}\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,0},

\displaystyle\beta_{\ell,3}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{\ell,3}+\beta_{\ell-1,3}\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,0}+\beta_{\ell-1,1}\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,1},

with

\displaystyle\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,0}={\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\right]^{2},\quad\dot{\alpha}_{\ell,1}={\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{\ell}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\right]^{2}\alpha_{\ell-1,1},

as in Lemma
3
.
This concludes the proof of the non-diagonal entries of
\mathbf{K}_{\NTK,\ell}
.

On the diagonal.

We now evaluate the diagonal entries of
\mathbf{K}_{\NTK,\ell}
as

\displaystyle[\mathbf{K}_{\NTK,\ell}]_{ii}

\displaystyle=[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}]_{ii}+[\mathbf{K}_{\NTK,\ell-1}]_{ii}\cdot[\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\CK,\ell}]_{ii}=\tau_{\ell}^{2}+\kappa_{\ell-1}^{2}\dot{\tau}_{\ell}^{2}+O(p^{-1/2}),

as a consequence of Lemma
1
, Lemma
3
, and the induction hypothesis.
We thus obtain

\kappa_{\ell}^{2}=\tau_{\ell}^{2}+\kappa_{\ell-1}^{2}\dot{\tau}_{\ell}^{2},

(62)

with
\kappa_{0}=\tau_{0}=\sqrt{\tr\mathbf{C}^{\circ}/p}
defined in Assumption
1
, where we recall
\dot{\tau}_{0}=0
from Lemma
3
.

Assembling in matrix form.

Putting everything together in matrix form, we obtain, as in the proof of Theorem
1
in Appendix
A.1
that

[\mathbf{K}_{\NTK,\ell}]_{ij}=\beta_{\ell,1}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{x}_{j}+\beta_{\ell,2}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})(t_{j}+\psi_{j})+\beta_{\ell,3}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2}),

and

[\mathbf{K}_{\NTK,\ell}]_{ii}=[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,\ell}]_{ii}+[\mathbf{K}_{\NTK,\ell-1}]_{ii}\cdot[\mathbf{K}^{\prime}_{\CK,\ell}]_{ii}=\tau_{\ell}^{2}+\kappa_{\ell-1}^{2}\dot{\tau}_{\ell}^{2}+O(p^{-1/2})\equiv\kappa_{\ell}^{2}+O(p^{-1/2}),

so that in matrix form

\mathbf{K}_{\NTK,\ell}=\beta_{\ell,1}\mathbf{X}^{\sf T}\mathbf{X}+\mathbf{V}\mathbf{B}_{\ell}\mathbf{V}^{\sf T}+(\kappa_{\ell}^{2}-\tau_{0}^{2}\beta_{\ell,1})\mathbf{I}_{n}+O_{\|\cdot\|}(p^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

(63)

where
O_{\|\cdot\|}(p^{-1/2})
denotes matrices of spectral norm order
O(p^{-1/2})
as
n,p\to\infty
, with

\mathbf{V}=[\mathbf{J}/\sqrt{p},~\boldsymbol{\psi}]\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times(K+1)},\quad\mathbf{B}_{\ell}=\begin{bmatrix}\beta_{\ell,2}\mathbf{t}\mathbf{t}^{\sf T}+\beta_{\ell,3}\mathbf{T}&\beta_{\ell,2}\mathbf{t}\\
\beta_{\ell,2}\mathbf{t}^{\sf T}&\beta_{\ell,2}\end{bmatrix},

(64)

which concludes the proof of Theorem
2
.

A.3
Two equivalent centering approaches in the single-hidden-layer case

In this section, we aim to show that “centering” the CK matrices
\mathbf{K}_{\CK}
by pre- and post-multiplying
\mathbf{P}=\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{1}_{n}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\sf T}/n
performed in
[
4
, Theorem 1]
is
equivalent
to take
{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma(\tau_{0}\xi)]=0
as in our Theorem
1
in the single-hidden-layer
\ell=1
setting, in the sense that one has

\|\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{K}_{\CK,1}-\tilde{\mathbf{K}}_{\CK,1})\mathbf{P}\|\to 0,

(65)

almost surely as
n,p\to\infty
, for the
same

\tilde{\mathbf{K}}_{\CK,1}
as defined in Theorem
1
and an
arbitrary
choice of
{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma(\tau_{0}\xi)]
(so in particular, one may freely take
{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma(\tau_{0}\xi)]\neq 0
which is different from the setting of our Theorem
1
).
The proof is as follows.

First note that the assumption
{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma(\tau_{0}\xi)]=0
is
only
used for the off-diagonal entries of the CK matrix
\mathbf{K}_{\CK,1}
, so we focus, in the sequel, only on the off-diagonal terms, while the discussions on the on-diagonal entries are the same as in Appendix
A.1
.

By its definition in (
5
) and the fact that
\mathbf{K}_{\CK,0}=\mathbf{X}^{\sf T}\mathbf{X}
, one has

[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,1}]_{ij}={\mathbb{E}}_{u,v}[\sigma_{1}(u)\sigma_{1}(v)],~\text{with}~u,v\sim{\mathcal{N}}\left(\mathbf{0},\begin{bmatrix}\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|^{2}&\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{x}_{j}\\
\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{x}_{j}&\|\mathbf{x}_{j}\|^{2}\end{bmatrix}\right),

(66)

so by performing a Gram-Schmidt orthogonalization procedure as in the proof of Theorem
1
in Appendix
A.1
, one has

u=\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|\cdot\xi_{i},\quad v=\|\mathbf{x}_{j}\|\left(\angle_{ij}\cdot\xi_{i}+\sqrt{1-\angle_{ij}^{2}}\cdot\xi_{j}\right),

(67)

for two
independent
standard Gaussian random variables
\xi_{i}
and
\xi_{j}
, where we denote the shortcut
\angle_{ij}\equiv\frac{\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{x}_{j}}{\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|\cdot\|\mathbf{x}_{j}\|}
for the “angle” between data vectors
\mathbf{x}_{i}
and
\mathbf{x}_{j}
.

It can be checked, for
\mathbf{x}_{i}=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}/\sqrt{p}+\mathbf{z}_{i}/\sqrt{p}
with
{\mathbb{E}}[\mathbf{z}_{i}]=\mathbf{0}
and
{\mathbb{E}}[\mathbf{z}_{i}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}]=\mathbf{C}_{i}
that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p}(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}+\mathbf{z}_{i})^{\sf T}(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}+\mathbf{z}_{i})=\frac{1}{p}\|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}\|^{2}+\frac{2}{p}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{i}+\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p}\|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}\|^{2}+\underbrace{\frac{2}{p}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{i}}_{O(p^{-1})}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{p}\tr\mathbf{C}^{\circ}}_{\equiv\tau_{0}^{2}=O(1)}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{p}\tr\mathbf{C}^{\circ}_{i}}_{\equiv t_{i}=O(p^{-1/2})}+\underbrace{\psi_{i}}_{O(p^{-1/2})},

where we recall the definition
\psi_{i}\equiv\frac{1}{p}\|\mathbf{z}_{i}\|^{2}-\frac{1}{p}\tr\mathbf{C}_{i}=O(p^{-1/2})
.
As such, by Taylor-expanding
\sqrt{\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|^{2}}
around
\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|^{2}\simeq\tau_{0}^{2}=O(1)
, we get

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|

\displaystyle=\tau_{0}+\frac{1}{2\tau_{0}}(\|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}\|^{2}/p+2\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{i}/p+t_{i}+\psi_{i})-\frac{1}{8\tau_{0}^{3}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}+O(p^{-3/2})

\displaystyle\equiv\tau_{0}+\theta_{i}+O(p^{-3/2}),

where we denote the shortcut

\theta_{i}\equiv\frac{1}{2\tau_{0}}(\|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}\|^{2}/p+2\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{i}/p+t_{i}+\psi_{i})-\frac{1}{8\tau_{0}^{3}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}=O(p^{-1/2}),

(68)

so that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{x}_{j}\|\angle_{ij}

\displaystyle=\frac{\frac{1}{p}(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}+\mathbf{z}_{i})^{\sf T}(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{j}+\mathbf{z}_{j})}{\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|}

\displaystyle=\frac{\frac{1}{p}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}^{\sf T}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{j}+\frac{1}{p}(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}+\boldsymbol{\mu}_{j}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{i})+\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tau_{0}+\frac{1}{2\tau_{0}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})+O(p^{-1})}+O(p^{-3/2})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}-\frac{t_{i}+\psi_{i}}{2\tau_{0}^{3}}+O(p^{-1})\right)\left(\frac{1}{p}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}^{\sf T}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{j}+\frac{1}{p}(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}+\boldsymbol{\mu}_{j}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{i})+\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)+O(p^{-3/2})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}+\frac{1}{p}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}^{\sf T}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{j}+\frac{1}{p}(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}+\boldsymbol{\mu}_{j}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{i})+S_{ij}\right)+O(p^{-3/2})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}A_{ij}+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2}).

Therefore, again by Taylor-expansion,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\|\mathbf{x}_{j}\|^{2}-(\|\mathbf{x}_{j}\|\angle_{ij})^{2}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{(\|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{j}\|^{2}/p+2\boldsymbol{\mu}_{j}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}/p+\tau_{0}^{2}+t_{j}+\psi_{j})-(A_{ij}/\tau_{0}+S_{ij})^{2}}

\displaystyle=\tau_{0}+\frac{1}{2\tau_{0}}(\|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}\|^{2}/p+2\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{i}/p+t_{i}+\psi_{i})-\frac{1}{8\tau_{0}^{3}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\tau_{0}^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2})

\displaystyle=\tau_{0}+\theta_{j}-\frac{1}{2\tau_{0}^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2}).

Following the same idea, we again Taylor-expand
\sigma_{1}(\cdot)
in the definition of
\mathbf{K}_{\CK,1}
as

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}(u)

\displaystyle=\sigma_{1}(\tau_{0}\xi_{i})+\sigma_{1}^{\prime}(\tau_{0}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}\theta_{i}+\frac{1}{8\tau_{0}^{2}}\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{0}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}^{2}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}+O(p^{-3/2}),

and

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}(v)

\displaystyle=\sigma_{1}\left(\|\mathbf{x}_{j}\|\angle_{ij}\xi_{j}+\|\mathbf{x}_{j}\|\sqrt{1-\angle_{ij}^{2}}\xi_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=\sigma_{1}\left(\tau_{0}\xi_{j}+\xi_{j}\theta_{j}-\xi_{j}\frac{1}{2\tau_{0}^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}+\xi_{i}\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}A_{ij}+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2})\right)

\displaystyle=\sigma_{1}(\tau_{0}\xi_{j})+\sigma_{1}^{\prime}(\tau_{0}\xi_{j})\xi_{j}\theta_{j}+\frac{1}{8\tau_{0}^{2}}\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{0}\xi_{j})\xi_{j}^{2}(t_{j}+\psi_{j})^{2}+X_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2}),

with

\displaystyle X_{ij}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\xi_{i}\sigma_{1}^{\prime}(\tau_{0}\xi_{j})A_{ij}+\frac{1}{2\tau_{0}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}\left(\xi_{i}^{2}\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{0}\xi_{j})-\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\xi_{j}\sigma_{1}^{\prime}(\tau_{0}\xi_{j})\right)+S_{ij}=O(p^{-1/2}),

where we recall the definition

A_{ij}=\underbrace{\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}}_{O(p^{-1/2})}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{p}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}^{\sf T}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{j}+\frac{1}{p}(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}+\boldsymbol{\mu}_{j}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{i})}_{O(p^{-1})}=\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{x}_{j}.

(69)

For independent
\xi_{i}
and
\xi_{j}
, we denote the following coefficients

p_{0}={\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{1}(\tau_{0}\xi)],\quad p_{1}={\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{1}^{\prime}(\tau_{0}\xi)],\quad p_{2}={\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{0}\xi)],\quad p_{3}={\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\tau_{0}\xi)],

(70)

so that

{\mathbb{E}}[\xi\sigma_{1}(\tau_{0}\xi)]=\tau_{0}{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{1}^{\prime}(\tau_{0}\xi)]=\tau_{0}p_{1},\quad{\mathbb{E}}[\xi\sigma_{1}^{\prime}(\tau_{0}\xi)]=\tau_{0}p_{2},

(71)

as well as

{\mathbb{E}}[\xi^{2}\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{0}\xi)]={\mathbb{E}}[(\xi^{2}-1)\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{0}\xi)]+p_{2}=\tau_{0}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(\tau_{0}\xi)]+p_{2}\equiv\tau_{0}^{2}p_{4}+p_{2},

(72)

for
p_{4}={\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(\tau_{0}\xi)]
.

This further allows us to write, for
A_{ij}=O(p^{-1/2})
and
\theta_{j}=O(p^{-1/2})
that

\displaystyle[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,1}]_{ij}={\mathbb{E}}_{u,v}[\sigma_{1}(u)\sigma_{1}(v)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{1}(\tau_{0}\xi_{i})+\sigma_{1}^{\prime}(\tau_{0}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}\theta_{i}+\frac{1}{8\tau_{0}^{2}}\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{0}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}^{2}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\times{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{1}(\tau_{0}\xi_{j})+\sigma_{1}^{\prime}(\tau_{0}\xi_{j})\xi_{j}\theta_{j}+\frac{1}{8\tau_{0}^{2}}\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{0}\xi_{j})\xi_{j}^{2}(t_{j}+\psi_{j})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(\sigma_{1}(\tau_{0}\xi_{i})+\sigma_{1}^{\prime}(\tau_{0}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}\theta_{i}+\frac{1}{8\tau_{0}^{2}}\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{0}\xi_{i})\xi_{i}^{2}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}\right)X_{ij}\right]+O(p^{-3/2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(p_{0}+\tau_{0}p_{2}\theta_{i}+\frac{\tau_{0}^{2}p_{4}+p_{2}}{8\tau_{0}^{2}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}\right)\left(p_{0}+\tau_{0}p_{2}\theta_{j}+\frac{\tau_{0}^{2}p_{4}+p_{2}}{8\tau_{0}^{2}}(t_{j}+\psi_{j})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sigma_{1}(\tau_{0}\xi_{i})X_{ij}\right]+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2}),

where the expectation is taken with respect to the
independent

\xi_{i}
and
\xi_{j}
(so, in fact, conditioned on
\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{x}_{j}
), so that

\displaystyle[\mathbf{K}_{\CK,1}]_{ij}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{u,v}[\sigma_{1}(u)\sigma_{1}(v)]

\displaystyle=\left(p_{0}+\tau_{0}p_{2}\theta_{i}+\frac{\tau_{0}^{2}p_{4}+p_{2}}{8\tau_{0}^{2}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}\right)\left(p_{0}+\tau_{0}p_{2}\theta_{j}+\frac{\tau_{0}^{2}p_{4}+p_{2}}{8\tau_{0}^{2}}(t_{j}+\psi_{j})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\xi_{i}\sigma_{1}(\tau_{0}\xi_{i})\sigma_{1}^{\prime}(\tau_{0}\xi_{j})A_{ij}+\frac{1}{2\tau_{0}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\left.\left(\xi_{i}^{2}\sigma_{1}(\tau_{0}\xi_{i})\sigma_{1}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{0}\xi_{j})-\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\sigma_{1}(\tau_{0}\xi_{i})\xi_{j}\sigma_{1}^{\prime}(\tau_{0}\xi_{j})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2})

\displaystyle=\left(p_{0}+\frac{p_{2}}{2}\chi_{i}+\frac{p_{4}}{8}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}\right)\left(p_{0}+\frac{p_{2}}{2}\chi_{j}+\frac{p_{4}}{8}(t_{j}+\psi_{j})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+p_{1}^{2}A_{ij}+\frac{1}{2\tau_{0}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}\cdot p_{2}\left({\mathbb{E}}[(\xi^{2}-1)\sigma_{1}(\tau_{0}\xi)]\right)+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2})

\displaystyle=p_{0}^{2}+\frac{p_{0}p_{2}}{2}(\chi_{i}+\chi_{j})+\frac{p_{0}p_{4}}{8}\left((t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}+(t_{j}+\psi_{j})^{2}\right)+\frac{p_{2}^{2}}{4}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})(t_{j}+\psi_{j})

\displaystyle\quad+p_{1}^{2}A_{ij}+\frac{p_{2}^{2}}{2}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)^{2}+S_{ij}+O(p^{-3/2}),

where we recall the shortcut

\theta_{i}\equiv\frac{1}{2\tau_{0}}(\|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}\|^{2}/p+2\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{i}/p+t_{i}+\psi_{i})-\frac{1}{8\tau_{0}^{2}\tau_{0}}(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}\equiv\frac{\chi_{i}}{2\tau_{0}}-\frac{(t_{i}+\psi_{i})^{2}}{8\tau_{0}^{2}\tau_{0}}=O(p^{-1/2}),

(73)

with

\chi_{i}\equiv t_{i}+\psi_{i}+\|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}\|^{2}/p+2\boldsymbol{\mu}_{i}^{\sf T}\mathbf{z}_{i}/p=\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|^{2}-\tau_{0}.

(74)

This gives, in matrix form,

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{\CK,1}

\displaystyle=p_{0}^{2}\mathbf{1}_{n}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\sf T}+p_{1}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{Z}^{\sf T}\mathbf{Z}+\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{J}\mathbf{M}^{\sf T}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{J}^{\sf T}+\frac{1}{p}(\mathbf{J}\mathbf{M}^{\sf T}\mathbf{Z}+\mathbf{Z}^{\sf T}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{J}^{\sf T})\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{p_{0}p_{2}}{2}(\boldsymbol{\chi}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\sf T}+\mathbf{1}_{n}\boldsymbol{\chi}^{\sf T})+\frac{p_{0}p_{4}}{8}\left((\{t_{a}\mathbf{1}_{n_{a}}\}_{a=1}^{K}+\boldsymbol{\psi})^{2}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\sf T}+\mathbf{1}_{n}[(\{t_{a}\mathbf{1}_{n_{a}}\}_{a=1}^{K}+\boldsymbol{\psi})^{2}]^{\sf T}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{p_{2}^{2}}{4}(\{t_{a}\mathbf{1}_{n_{a}}\}_{a=1}^{K}+\boldsymbol{\psi})(\{t_{a}\mathbf{1}_{n_{a}}\}_{a=1}^{K}+\boldsymbol{\psi})^{\sf T}+\frac{p_{2}^{2}}{2}\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{Z}^{\sf T}\mathbf{Z}\right)^{\circ 2}

\displaystyle+({\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{1}^{2}(\tau_{0}\xi)]-p_{0}^{2}-\tau_{0}^{2}p_{1}^{2})\mathbf{I}_{n}+O_{\|\cdot\|}(p^{-1/2}),

where we denote
\boldsymbol{\chi}\equiv\{\chi_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
\mathbf{A}^{\circ 2}
the
entry-wise
square of the matrix
\mathbf{A}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n}
, i.e.,
[\mathbf{A}^{\circ 2}]_{ij}=[\mathbf{A}_{ij}]^{2}
, and use again the fact that
\|\mathbf{A}\|\leq n\|\mathbf{A}\|_{\infty}
for
\mathbf{A}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n}
with
\|\mathbf{A}\|_{\infty}=\max_{ij}|\mathbf{A}|_{ij}
,
\{S_{ij}\}_{i,j}=O_{\|\cdot\|}(p^{-\frac{1}{2}})
as well as
\left(\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{Z}^{\sf T}\mathbf{Z}\right)^{\circ 2}=\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{J}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{J}^{\sf T}+O_{\|\cdot\|}(p^{-1/2})
according to
[
11
]
.

Finally, using the fact that for
\mathbf{P}=\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{1}_{n}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\sf T}/n
, we have
\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\sf T}\mathbf{P}=\mathbf{0},\mathbf{P}\mathbf{1}_{n}=\mathbf{0}
, we conclude the proof of (
65
) with the same expression of
\tilde{\mathbf{K}}_{\CK,1}
as in the statement of our Theorem
1
,
without
the assumption
{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{1}(\tau_{0}\xi)]=0
.
This, however, no longer holds in the multi-layer setting with a number of layers
L\geq 1
.

A.4
Proof and discussions of Corollary
1

To prove Corollary
1
, it can be easily checked that the i.i.d. entries of the weights
\mathbf{W}
defined in (
18
) have zero mean and unit variance. So we focus on the design of the activations.

To ensure that the activation functions
\sigma_{\ell}(\cdot)
s are “centered” and satisfy
{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]=0
, we define, with a slight abuse of notation, for the non-negative sequence
\tau_{1},\ldots,\tau_{L}
defined in Theorem
1
,

\sigma_{T}(t)=a\cdot(1_{t<s_{1}}+1_{t>s_{2}}),\quad\sigma_{Q}(t)=b_{1}\cdot(1_{t<r_{1}}+1_{t>r_{4}})+b_{2}\cdot 1_{r_{2}\leq t\leq r_{3}},

(75)

and take
\alpha_{\ell,0}\equiv{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{T}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]
,
\sigma_{T}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)\equiv\tilde{\sigma}_{T}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)=\sigma_{T}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)-\alpha_{\ell,0}
, which serves as the activation of the first
\ell=1,\ldots,L-1
layers, and
a
,
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
satisfying the following equations

{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{T}^{\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]=\frac{a}{\sqrt{2\pi}\tau_{\ell-1}}\cdot\left(e^{-s_{2}^{2}/(2\tau^{2}_{\ell-1})}-e^{-s_{1}^{2}/(2\tau^{2}_{\ell-1})}\right),

(76)

{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{T}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]=\frac{a}{\sqrt{2\pi}\tau^{3}_{\ell-1}}\cdot\left(s_{2}e^{-s_{2}^{2}/(2\tau^{2}_{\ell-1})}-s_{1}e^{-s_{1}^{2}/(2\tau^{2}_{\ell-1})}\right),

(77)

{\mathbb{E}}[(\sigma_{T}^{2}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi))^{\prime\prime}]=\frac{a^{2}-2a\cdot\alpha_{\ell,0}}{\sqrt{2\pi}\tau^{3}_{\ell-1}}\cdot\left(s_{2}e^{-s_{2}^{2}/(2\tau^{2}_{\ell-1})}-s_{1}e^{-s_{1}^{2}/(2\tau^{2}_{\ell-1})}\right),

(78)

{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{T}^{2}(\tau_{\ell-1}\xi)]=\frac{a^{2}}{2}\left(\erf\left(\frac{s_{1}}{\sqrt{2}\tau_{\ell-1}}\right)-\erf\left(\frac{s_{2}}{\sqrt{2}\tau_{\ell-1}}\right)+2\right)-\alpha_{\ell,0}^{2},

(79)

and
\alpha_{L,0}\equiv{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{Q}(\tau\xi)]
,
\sigma_{T}(\tau\xi)\equiv\tilde{\sigma}_{T}(\tau\xi)=\sigma_{T}(\tau\xi)-\alpha_{L,0}
, which serves as the activation of the last and
L
th layer, and
b_{1}
,
b_{2}
,
r_{1}
,
r_{2}
,
r_{3}
and
r_{4}
satisfying the following equations

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{Q}^{\prime}(\tau\xi)]=\frac{b_{1}\left(e^{-r_{4}^{2}/(2\tau^{2})}-e^{-r_{2}^{2}/(2\tau^{2})}\right)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\tau}+\frac{b_{2}\left(e^{-r^{2}_{2}/(2\tau^{2})}-e^{-r^{3}_{2}/(2\tau^{2})}\right)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\tau},

(80)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\sigma_{Q}^{\prime\prime}(\tau\xi)]=\frac{b_{1}\left(r_{4}e^{-r_{4}^{2}/(2\tau^{2})}-r_{1}e^{-r_{1}^{2}/(2\tau^{2})}\right)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\tau^{3}}+\frac{b_{2}\left(r_{2}e^{-r^{2}_{2}/(2\tau^{2})}-r_{3}e^{-r^{3}_{2}/(2\tau^{2})}\right)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\tau^{3}},

(81)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[(\sigma_{Q}^{2}(\tau\xi))^{\prime\prime}]

\displaystyle=\frac{b_{1}^{2}\left(r_{4}e^{-r_{4}^{2}/(2\tau^{2})}-r_{1}e^{-r_{1}^{2}/(2\tau^{2})}\right)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\tau^{3}}+\frac{b_{2}^{2}\left(r_{2}e^{-r^{2}_{2}/(2\tau^{2})}-r_{3}e^{-r^{3}_{2}/(2\tau^{2})}\right)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\tau^{3}},

(82)

\displaystyle-2\alpha_{L,0}{\mathbb{E}}[(\sigma_{Q}^{\prime\prime}(\tau\xi))],

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[(\sigma_{Q}^{2}(\tau\xi))]

\displaystyle=\frac{b_{1}^{2}}{2}\left(\erf\left(\frac{r_{1}}{\sqrt{2}\tau}\right)-\erf\left(\frac{r_{4}}{\sqrt{2}\tau}\right)\right)+b_{1}^{2}+\frac{b_{2}^{2}}{2}\left(\erf\left(\frac{r_{2}}{\sqrt{2}\tau}\right)-\erf\left(\frac{r_{3}}{\sqrt{2}\tau}\right)\right)

(83)

\displaystyle-\alpha_{L,0}^{2}

with
\tau=\tau_{L-1}
.

A few remarks on Corollary
1
and Algorithm
1
are as follows.

On the numerical determinations of
\sigma_{T}
and
\sigma_{Q}
.

The above system of nonlinear equations does not admit explicit solutions, but can be solved efficiently using, for example, a (numerical) least squares method.
Precisely, we use the numerical least squares method (the
optimize.minimize
function of
SciPy
library) to solve the above system of equations, and run for
1\,000
times with random and independent initializations to get
1\,000
solutions, among which we choose the optimal parameters to determine
\sigma_{Q}
and
\sigma_{T}
.

On the two activations.

Note that in Algorithm
1
we use the activation
\sigma_{T}
and
\sigma_{Q}
respectively for the first
\ell=1,\ldots,L-1
and the final and
L
th layer, since we
only
need to match the key parameters
\alpha_{\ell,1}
,
\alpha_{\ell,2}
and
\alpha_{\ell,3}
for the first
\ell=1,\ldots,L-1
layer, and the additional parameter
\tau_{\ell}
for the last
L
th, so as to obtain spectrally equivalent CK and NTK matrices for the whole network of depth
L
.
Also note that the proposed activation functions
\sigma_{T}
and
\sigma_{Q}
have respectively three and five (in fact six parameters with the symmetric constraint
r_{1}-r_{2}=r_{3}-r_{4}
as in Figure
1
) parameters that are freely tunable.
And we have respectively three and four (nonlinear) equations to determine these parameters in the system of equations above.

Appendix B
Additional experiments

In this section, we provide additional experiments to demonstrate the advantageous performance of the proposed “lossless” compression approach.
Figure
4
depicts the classification accuracies using three different neural networks: (i) the original “dense and unquantized” nets with three fully-connected layers of
\ReLU
activations, (ii) the proposed sparse and quantized DNN model as per Algorithm
1
, and (iii) the “heuristically” compressed networks by (iii-i) uniformly and randomly zeroing out
90\%
of the weights, as well as (iii-ii) natively binarizing using
\sigma(t)=1_{t<-1}+1_{t>1}
, on two tasks of MNIST data classification
[
32
]
having five classes (digits
0,1,2,3,4
) and two classes (digits
6
versus
8
).
This allows us to have a more qualitative assessment of the impact of data and task on the performance of the proposed compression scheme.
We see, as in Figure
3
for ten-class MNIST and ten-class CIFAR10, that the proposed compression approach significantly outperform the two “naive” compression approaches, and can achieve a memory compression rate of
10^{3}
and a level of sparsity up to
90\%
, with virtually no performance loss.
Also note that the experimental settings of Figure
4
is almost the
same
as those of Figure
3
in Section
4
, except that the former networks have less neurons per layer and slightly higher level of sparsity (
90\%
here instead of
80\%
in the setting of Figure
3
), to solve the simpler two-class or five-class classification problems.
